### Day 26 — 15 June 2026 (Monday)

**Progress:**
Today's work on HomeSewa focused entirely on UI polish, form improvements, Admin Login redesign, push notification configuration, and version control setup. All changes were pushed to both GitHub repositories.

**Contact Page — Map Pin Overlay:**
Added a red location pin (`Ionicons name="location"`) absolutely positioned over the static Google Maps image on the Contact page. The pin is positioned at 30% top, 50% left with a translateX offset to centre it precisely over the Kamalpokhari area. This was implemented as a pure React Native overlay with `pointerEvents="none"` so it does not interfere with the "Open in Maps" button below.

**Form UI Fixes — Career.tsx & Partnership.tsx:**
Moved the Gender label and Male/Female radio buttons onto the same row by wrapping them in a `genderRow` flex container with `alignItems: 'center'`. Added a space before `*` in all required field labels across Career, Partnership, and Book forms — a detail that had been inconsistent across the codebase. Changed phone number formatting from the 3-3-4 pattern (985 202 4365) to the 5-2-3 pattern (98520 24 365) across all phone fields in Career (three fields: phone, emergency, referral), Partnership, and Book. Fixed the keyboard covering the Message/TextArea on Android by adding `enableOnAndroid={true}` and increasing `extraScrollHeight` from 80 to 120 on all `KeyboardAwareScrollView` instances — the missing `enableOnAndroid` flag was the root cause of Android keyboard behaviour not triggering scroll.

**FileUploadBox Component:**
Removed the `+ Add more` dashed button that appeared alongside uploaded files — the user confirmed this was not needed. Fixed `mediaTypes: 'images'` to `mediaTypes: ['images']` (newer versions of expo-image-picker require an array).

**DropdownAdd Component:**
Replaced the `+` image icon with an `Ionicons` chevron icon (`chevron-up` / `chevron-down`) that toggles based on the `isFocus` state. Removed the `Image` import and the `getDropIcon` helper function that were no longer needed. Also removed the `DropIconAdd` asset require.

**Admin Login Page — Full Redesign:**
Completely rewrote AdminLogin.tsx with a next-level design matching the HomeSewa corporate identity. Key changes: removed `ScrollView` — the page is now a single-screen `KeyboardAvoidingView` with no scrolling; added `Header4` navigation bar pinned at the top; added a `LinearGradient` brand area (logo circle, HomeSewa name, "ADMIN PORTAL" label) between the header and the login card; applied corporate green `#295C59` throughout inputs, button, and link accents; phone number input now formats to 5-2-3 pattern; Logout button is now conditionally rendered only when `auth.currentUser` is active via `onAuthStateChanged`; removed the "Create Account" link; updated navigation links to "Become a Member : Join Now" (routes to /Partnership) and "Join as Professional : Join Now" (routes to /Career) with a space after the colon as required; replaced all image-based icons (PhoneIcon, KeyIcon, EyeOffIcon, EyeOnIcon) with `Ionicons`; fixed Android keyboard behaviour by setting `behavior={undefined}` instead of `behavior="height"` which was shrinking the card layout. Also fixed a routing bug where "Join as Professional" was incorrectly navigating to /Partnership instead of /Career.

**Tab Bar:**
Changed `tabBarActiveTintColor` from the generic CSS `'green'` string to the corporate HomeSewa green `#295C59` in `app/(drawer)/(tabs)/_layout.tsx`.

**OneSignal Push Notification Configuration:**
Changed OneSignal plugin mode from `"development"` to `"production"` in app.json. Added the `appId` field directly into the onesignal-expo-plugin config in app.json so it is baked into the APK during build. Updated the OneSignal App ID to `6088a935-58f5-4645-8290-80633f2bdbc5` (new HomeSewa OneSignal app). Configured the Firebase Service Account JSON in the OneSignal dashboard under Google Android (FCM) configuration — a required step for Android push delivery. Identified that old APK builds will not receive notifications and a rebuild is required.

**GitHub Version Control:**
Set up two remote repositories and pushed the full codebase to both `https://github.com/SRIYOGconsulting/HomeSewa` and `https://github.com/Samek55/HomeSewa`. Resolved branch naming issues (local branch was `master`, remote default was `main`) using force push. Deleted the stale `master` branch from both remotes after confirming `main` was up to date. Added `INTERNSHIP_REPORT.md` to `.gitignore` to keep the internship log local only.

**Bug Fixed:**
Fixed a `[ReferenceError: Property 'Image' doesn't exist]` crash in DropdownAdd.tsx caused by a stale Metro bundle still referencing the removed `Image` import. Resolved by clearing the Metro cache with `npx expo start --clear`.

**Learning:**
`enableOnAndroid={true}` is a non-obvious but critical prop for `KeyboardAwareScrollView` — without it, the component silently does nothing on Android and inputs at the bottom of forms remain hidden under the keyboard. Learned that OneSignal's `mode: "development"` in app.json restricts notification delivery and must be changed to `"production"` before real-device testing. Understood that the OneSignal `appId` must be present both in `.env` (for JS runtime) and in `app.json` plugin config (for native Android baking during prebuild). Force pushing to GitHub (`--force`) overwrites remote history — appropriate when the remote has divergent commits from an unrelated initialisation, but must be used carefully on shared repositories.

**Skills Gained:**
React Native forms — phone number formatting with regex slice and conditional string concatenation, `KeyboardAwareScrollView` Android configuration · UI design — corporate identity application across login screens, conditional rendering based on auth state, `LinearGradient` brand headers · Component refactoring — replacing image assets with icon libraries, removing unused imports · OneSignal — FCM service account configuration, production mode setup, appId native baking · Git — multi-remote push workflow, branch renaming, force push, `.gitignore` for sensitive files

**Challenge & Solution:**
(1) Phone formatting 5-2-3 vs 3-3-4: The existing formatting logic used `slice(0,3)` breakpoints. Required rewriting all three conditional branches across six fields in two files to use `slice(0,5)` and `slice(5,7)` breakpoints. (2) Admin Login keyboard on Android: `KeyboardAvoidingView` with `behavior="height"` was resizing and collapsing the card when the keyboard appeared. Fix: use `behavior={undefined}` on Android so the layout stays fixed and the keyboard simply overlays — acceptable since the form is short and all inputs are visible above the keyboard. (3) `Image` ReferenceError after DropdownAdd refactor: Metro hot-reload served a stale bundle still containing the old `Image` reference. Fix: full Metro cache clear.

**Message to Mentor:**
Today demonstrated that UI polish and form consistency work — while less glamorous than feature development — has a direct and visible impact on the professionalism of the product. Standardising phone formats, label spacing, and keyboard behaviour across all forms makes the app feel cohesive and production-ready. The Admin Login redesign in particular showed me how much design quality can be elevated simply by applying a consistent colour system, replacing generic placeholder icons with brand-aligned alternatives, and removing unnecessary UI elements. I am increasingly confident in making independent design decisions that align with the project's visual identity.

### Day 27 — 16 June 2026 (Tuesday)

**Progress:**
Today's work was a full rebrand pass on HomeSewa following HR sign-off on the final logo artwork (`HomeSewa-Logo.png` — a teal praying-hands-and-house mark replacing the previous black-and-white version), followed by a round of native bug fixes surfaced only once the new build was actually run on a device.

**Logo rollout — app icon, adaptive icon, splash screens (Android + iOS):**
Replaced the master logo assets (`homesewa.png`, `homesewa-icon-fg.png`) and regenerated every derived asset from the single new source file: the Android native launcher icons (`ic_launcher.webp` / `ic_launcher_round.webp` / `ic_launcher_foreground.webp` across `mdpi` through `xxxhdpi`), the Android splash drawables, the iOS `AppIcon.appiconset` 1024×1024 icon, and the iOS `SplashScreenLogo.imageset` (1x/2x/3x). Since neither `sharp` nor ImageMagick was available in this environment, I located a working Python 3.13 + Pillow install on the machine and wrote a small generation script that crops/pads the source logo to match the exact fill-ratio "pattern" the previous artwork used at each size, rather than just dropping the raw file in everywhere.

**Android 12+ splash-icon clipping fix:**
The first real-device build showed only the praying hands on the splash screen — the roof and chimney were being clipped off. Root cause: `windowSplashScreenAnimatedIcon` on Android 12+ enforces the same circular safe-zone masking as an adaptive icon, and the splash artwork had been generated at a "full-bleed" ~94% fill (matching the old artwork's pattern), which is too large for that safe zone once the logo isn't itself a perfect circle. Fixed by regenerating the splash drawables at the same ~58% safe-zone fill ratio already proven correct for the adaptive icon foreground, confirmed by rebuilding and checking the splash on-device.

**Header consistency — circular logo badge + WhatsApp icon:**
The new logo art has no circle baked into it (unlike the old one), so it rendered as a free-floating shape in the dark-green header bars instead of the expected circular badge. Wrapped the logo `Image` in a 42×42 white circular `View` (`borderRadius: 21`, `overflow: 'hidden'`) across `Header1`, `Header2`, `Header3drawer`, and `Header4Admin`, restoring the original circular look. While auditing the headers I also found the WhatsApp shortcut button only existed in `Header1` — added the same button consistently to the other three so every header nav bar behaves identically.

**Splash-flash investigation and fix:**
Asked to confirm how many splash screens existed, I traced the boot sequence and found two unbranded loading states that could flash between the native splash hiding and the app being ready: a white screen with a green `ActivityIndicator` in `RootLayout`, and a blank `null` render in `app/index.tsx` while it read the onboarding flag from `AsyncStorage`. Both screens called `SplashScreen.hideAsync()` independently — whichever async check (Firebase auth vs. the onboarding lookup) finished first hid the splash prematurely, exposing the other, unbranded screen underneath. Built a small shared gate (`src/utils/splashGate.ts`) that only hides the native splash once **both** checks have reported in via `markSplashReady()`, with a 6-second safety-net timeout in case a future flow skips one of the checks, and removed the spinner UI from `_layout.tsx` entirely since it should now never be visible.

**OneSignal notification icon fix:**
A test push showed the small status-bar icon as a near-solid green blob and the large expanded-notification icon still showing the old black logo. Investigating the small icon, I found the real cause: Android notification small icons use *only* the alpha channel to build a monochrome silhouette, and the new logo's "white" interior was actually fully opaque rather than transparent — so the entire rounded-square plate behind the line art was being treated as filled icon content. Fixed by isolating just the teal line-art pixels into a fresh alpha mask (discarding the opaque white plate) and regenerating `ic_stat_onesignal_default.png` at all five densities plus the `ic_onesignal_large_icon_default.png`, verifying each by compositing it onto a solid teal background before rebuilding.

**About page banner image fix:**
The About page banner showed a thin white line running around the photo. Pixel-level brightness analysis showed it wasn't a styling bug at all — it was two vertical artifact lines baked into the left and right edges of `Header.jpeg` itself (left over from however the source image had been generated), with no artifact on the top or bottom edges. Fixed by cropping the image a few pixels inside both lines and overwriting the asset directly.

**Admin Login — removed redundant brand block:**
Once rendered on a real device, the `LinearGradient` brand block added during yesterday's Admin Login redesign (lock icon, "HomeSewa", "ADMIN LOGIN") read as a redundant duplicate of the header directly above it rather than a polish element. Removed the block along with the now-unused `LinearGradient`/`Image` imports and associated styles, leaving the header nav bar flow straight into the "Sign In" card.

**Hardware back-button fix:**
Pressing the hardware back button from Home was unexpectedly landing on the FAQs screen instead of exiting the app. Root cause: `app/(drawer)/_layout.tsx`'s `<Drawer>` never declared an `initialRouteName`, and `FAQs` happened to be the first `<Drawer.Screen>` child declared in JSX — React Navigation silently treated it as the drawer's implicit initial route, and the boot-time redirect to `/Home` landed on top of that phantom route in navigation history. Fixed with two complementary changes: explicitly setting `initialRouteName="(tabs)"` on the Drawer, and adding a global `BackHandler` in `app/_layout.tsx` that collapses any back-press from inside the drawer/tabs area straight to `/Home`, and only exits the app once the user presses back again while already on Home — regardless of what raw navigation history happens to contain.

**Build verification:**
Each fix was verified incrementally with `npx expo run:android` against a `Pixel_6` emulator before moving to the next issue — icon/splash first, then the splash-gate change, then the notification icons, then the back-button fix — rather than batching everything into a single untested build.

**Learning:**
The biggest lesson today was that a logo swap is never "just replace the file" once an asset is consumed by multiple platform-level systems with their own hidden constraints: Android 12+'s SplashScreen API silently safe-zone-masks any animated icon exactly like an adaptive icon; Android's notification small-icon pipeline ignores RGB entirely and only reads alpha, so an "opaque white background" and a "transparent background" look identical in a normal image viewer but behave completely differently once Android processes them; and React Navigation's Drawer will quietly infer an initial route from JSX declaration order if you don't set one explicitly, which can plant a "phantom" screen in navigation history that only reveals itself when the user presses back. None of these were visible by inspecting the source files — each one only surfaced by actually rebuilding and looking at the rendered result on a device, reinforcing the value of verifying every asset/navigation change with a real build rather than trusting it by inspection alone.

**Skills Gained:**
Image processing with Pillow/NumPy — alpha-mask isolation from a flattened image, safe-zone fill-ratio calculation and reproduction across icon families · Android platform internals — SplashScreen API icon safe-zone masking, notification small-icon monochrome alpha-only rendering, adaptive icon foreground conventions · React Navigation — Drawer `initialRouteName` semantics, global hardware `BackHandler` patterns spanning multiple nested navigators · Debugging methodology — pixel-level brightness analysis to diagnose a baked-in image artifact, compositing transparent PNGs onto solid backgrounds to visually verify alpha-channel content before rebuilding native binaries.

**Challenge & Solution:**
(1) Splash screen showed only the praying hands, roof clipped off. Diagnosed as Android 12+'s SplashScreen API applying adaptive-icon-style safe-zone masking to a "full-bleed" splash image. Fixed by regenerating the splash artwork at the same ~58% safe-zone fill ratio already used for the adaptive icon foreground. (2) Notification small icon rendered as a solid green blob instead of a readable glyph. Diagnosed by compositing the generated icon onto a teal background and seeing a filled white disc rather than thin line art — traced to the source logo's white interior being opaque rather than transparent. Fixed by rebuilding the icon's alpha channel from only the colored line-art pixels. (3) Hardware back button landed on FAQs instead of exiting from Home. Diagnosed by tracing the Drawer's screen declaration order and finding `FAQs` was an undeclared "phantom" initial route. Fixed with an explicit `initialRouteName` plus a global `BackHandler` that always collapses to Home before allowing exit.

**Message to Mentor:**
Today reinforced that a rebrand is deceptively complex — every surface a logo touches (app icon, adaptive icon, splash, in-app header, push notification) has its own platform-specific rendering rules, and several of those rules only fail in ways that are invisible until you rebuild and look at a real device. I've adopted the habit of treating "the asset is updated" and "the asset renders correctly on-device" as two separate, equally necessary verification steps, and will carry that discipline into all future asset and navigation work on this project.

### Day 28 — 17 June 2026 (Wednesday)

**Progress:**
Today's work on HomeSewa covered a major OTP flow overhaul — migrating from Firebase Phone Authentication to Sparrow SMS Nepal — along with a full round of UI polish, a drawer restructure, Admin Login branding restoration, and Sparrow SMS API debugging that identified a blocking infrastructure issue.

**Sparrow SMS OTP Integration — BookingOtp.tsx and helpboxOTP.tsx:**
The HR team confirmed the project will use Sparrow SMS (Nepal) rather than Firebase Phone Auth for OTP delivery. Firebase Phone Auth enforces a 6-digit code and requires additional Google Play Integrity configuration — Sparrow SMS allows a simpler 4-digit flow with local verification. I rewrote both `BookingOtp.tsx` and `helpboxOTP.tsx` to remove all Firebase auth imports (`getAuth`, `signInWithPhoneNumber`, `globalFirebaseConfirmation`) and replaced the entire OTP send/verify pipeline. The new flow generates a 4-digit code client-side using `Math.floor(1000 + Math.random() * 9000)`, stores it in a `sentOtp` state variable, sends it to the user's Nepal number via the Sparrow SMS v2 API (`https://api.sparrowsms.com/v2/sms/`), and verifies it by comparing the entered value against the stored code on Submit. The Sparrow token was moved from a hardcoded string into `.env` as `EXPO_PUBLIC_SPARROW_TOKEN` so it is not checked into version control.

**4-digit OTP UI:**
Both OTP screens were updated from 6-box to 4-box inputs. Each box uses `width * 0.14` sizing, `maxLength={1}`, `keyboardType="numeric"`, and refs for auto-focus-next and backspace-to-previous navigation — the same keyboard UX pattern as the previous 6-digit implementation, adapted for the shorter code.

**BookingDetail.tsx — Firebase removal:**
`BookingDetail.tsx` previously called `globalBookingFirebaseConfirmation` to trigger Firebase phone auth before navigating to the OTP screen. Removed all Firebase imports, the `SubmitOverlay` modal, and the associated `useState` variables (`overlayVisible`, `overlayStatus`). The `handleSubmit` function now navigates directly to `BookingOtp` with all booking params.

**Service Ending Date — Book.tsx → BookingDetail.tsx → BookingOtp.tsx:**
Added a required Service Ending Date date picker to `Book.tsx` — labeled `Service Ending Date *` with `minimumDate` set to the selected start date so the end date cannot precede the start. Validation blocks submission if no end date is selected. The selected date is passed as `endDate` through `BookingDetail.tsx` (which displays both "Starting Date" and "Ending Date" rows in the summary card) and into `BookingOtp.tsx`, where it is saved to Airtable as `'Service Completion Date'` only when present.

**NumberBar.tsx — placeholder on focus:**
Added `isFocused` state to `NumberBar.tsx`. The placeholder text (`'98520 24 365'`) is cleared when the input is focused (`placeholder={isFocused ? '' : '98520 24 365'}`), so it does not compete visually with the user's typing.

**OTP Verified screen — auto-redirect:**
Added a `useEffect` with a 5-second `setTimeout` in `otpVerifiedHB.tsx` that calls `router.replace('/Home')` automatically, so users are returned to the Home screen without having to tap anything after verification. The timeout is cleared in the cleanup function to prevent `setState` on unmount.

**Header3drawer — back arrow:**
Added a back arrow `TouchableOpacity` to `Header3drawer.tsx` using `Ionicons name="arrow-back"` for screens accessed from the sidebar drawer (FAQs, Glossary, Become a Partner, Join as Professional) that have no footer tab bar navigation. Used `router.canGoBack() ? router.back() : router.replace('/Home')` to handle the case where a drawer screen is opened directly with no navigation history — without the `canGoBack()` guard, `router.back()` threw an unhandled GO_BACK error.

**Drawer reorder — CustomDrawer.tsx:**
Reordered the sidebar menu items so that "Book a Service" appears directly below "Services" and "Join as Professional" appears directly below "Book a Service", matching the logical user journey through the app.

**Admin Login — HomeSewa branding restored:**
Added back the lock icon and "HomeSewa / ADMIN LOGIN" branding block between the header bar and the sign-in card in `AdminLogin.tsx`, using a white circular ring container for the lock icon image (`assets/icons/admin/lock.png`) and styled `Text` components for the brand name and subtitle.

**Clear form button — corporate green:**
Updated the `clearIcon` `tintColor` in both `Partnership.tsx` and `Career.tsx` from the default colour to `#295C59` (HomeSewa corporate green) so the "Clear form" icon is consistent with the rest of the app's accent colour system.

**Sparrow SMS debugging — IP whitelist error:**
After the Sparrow SMS integration was in place and the Metro bundler was restarted (required to pick up the new `EXPO_PUBLIC_SPARROW_TOKEN` env variable), the user reported that OTP SMS was not arriving. I added response checking and console logging to `sendSparrowOtp` in both files — previously the `fetch` call discarded the response entirely, silently swallowing any API error since `fetch` does not throw on non-2xx responses. After the fix, the Metro console printed the full API response:

```
[Sparrow] token: v2_bIsagaGEqW6kvSg5hKq4JBSMJxJ.zRuf
[Sparrow] to: 9779843624971
[Sparrow] response status: 403 body: {"response_code":1001,"response":"Invalid IP Address","requested_ip":"43.245.86.60"}
```

The Sparrow SMS account has IP whitelisting enabled, and the request originating from the mobile device's carrier IP (`43.245.86.60`) was not on the whitelist. Since mobile apps do not have a fixed outbound IP address — the IP changes per device, carrier, and session — IP whitelisting is incompatible with direct mobile API calls. I escalated to HR with instructions to log into the Sparrow SMS portal and remove the IP restriction (or add `0.0.0.0/0` to allow all IPs).

**Committed and pushed:**
All changes were staged and committed with the message `"feat: Sparrow SMS OTP, service end date, drawer reorder, admin login polish"` and pushed to both `https://github.com/Samek55/HomeSewa` and `https://github.com/SRIYOGconsulting/HomeSewa`.

**Learning:**
The most important technical lesson today was that `fetch` does not throw on HTTP error responses — a `fetch` call that receives a `403 Forbidden` or `401 Unauthorized` will resolve normally and the `catch` block will never fire. This means any API integration that does not explicitly check `response.ok` or `response.status` is silently ignoring all server-side errors. The correct pattern is always: `const response = await fetch(...); const data = await response.json(); if (!response.ok) throw new Error(...)`. Without this, production API failures become invisible to both the developer and the user.

Learned also that IP whitelisting is a security control designed for server-to-server API calls where the calling machine has a fixed IP address — it is the wrong security model for direct mobile-to-API calls, where the IP is different for every user and every session. The correct architecture for production is to route sensitive third-party API calls through a backend server (where the fixed server IP can be whitelisted), keeping the API credentials entirely server-side. The current client-side approach also means the `EXPO_PUBLIC_SPARROW_TOKEN` is embedded in the app bundle and can be extracted by anyone who unpacks the APK.

**Skills Gained:**
React Native API integration — `fetch` response status checking, `response.json()` error body extraction, surfacing HTTP errors as meaningful `Alert` messages · Sparrow SMS Nepal v2 API — token-based authentication, phone number format (`977` + 10-digit), IP whitelist architecture · Expo env variables — `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` pattern, Metro bundler restart requirement after `.env` changes · React Native OTP UI — 4-digit box layout with auto-focus and backspace navigation · `expo-router` — `router.canGoBack()` guard for safe back navigation, `router.replace` for redirect flows · React Native UI — conditional placeholder on focus, `setTimeout` auto-redirect with `useEffect` cleanup

**Challenge & Solution:**
(1) OTP SMS not received after Sparrow SMS integration. Root cause: the `fetch` call was not checking the API response at all — the `403 Invalid IP Address` error from Sparrow was silently discarded. Fix: added `response.ok` check, `response.json()` parsing, and a `console.log` of the full response, which surfaced the exact error code and IP address. Escalated IP whitelist removal to HR. (2) `GO_BACK` navigation crash on drawer screens with no history. Root cause: `router.back()` called unconditionally on a screen opened with no prior navigation stack. Fix: `router.canGoBack() ? router.back() : router.replace('/Home')`.

**Message to Mentor:**
Today reinforced that debugging a silent failure is significantly harder than debugging a visible one. The OTP SMS issue could have taken hours to diagnose if the API call had simply "done nothing" with no response information surfaced. The immediate fix — adding response checking and logging — is a habit I will apply to every API integration from the start rather than adding it only when something breaks. Knowing exactly what the server is returning is always more valuable than inferring it from the user-visible symptom.

### Day 29 — 18 June 2026 (Thursday)

**Progress:**
Today was one of the most feature-dense sessions of the HomeSewa internship, covering OTP flows for two new forms, a full Supabase authentication migration, professional login provisioning, a work completion verification system, and a set of targeted UI fixes.

**OTP Verification for Career and Partnership forms:**
Previously, the "Join as Professional" and "Become a Partner" forms submitted directly to Supabase on button press with no phone verification. I added the same OTP gate already in place for bookings and helpbox. In `Career.tsx` and `Partnership.tsx`, the `handleSubmit` function now uploads images to Cloudinary, serialises the complete form data into AsyncStorage (`pendingCareerData` / `pendingPartnershipData`), then navigates to a new dedicated OTP screen instead of calling the API directly. I created `CareerOTP.tsx` and `PartnershipOTP.tsx` — both follow the same pattern as `helpboxOTP.tsx`: generate a 4-digit code on mount, send it via Sparrow SMS, display four box inputs with auto-focus-next and backspace-to-previous keyboard navigation, verify the entered code, read the pending data from AsyncStorage, call `createCareer` / `createPartnership`, remove the AsyncStorage key, and show the `SubmitOverlay` success state.

**Clear Form fix after OTP submission:**
After OTP verification succeeded the `SubmitOverlay` offered "Keep Form" and "Clear Form". Tapping "Clear Form" navigated back to the form screen but left all fields populated, because the form component's state was preserved across the navigation transition. Fixed by passing `{ clearForm: 'true' }` as a navigation param from the OTP screen's `onClear` handler and adding a `useEffect` in both `CareerScreen` and `PartnershipScreen` that calls the existing `clearAllFields()` function whenever `clearForm === 'true'` is detected via `useLocalSearchParams`.

**Headshot column added to workforce table:**
The submitted professional record showed `headshot: null` because the `workforce` table had no `headshot` column. Added `ALTER TABLE workforce ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS headshot TEXT` in Supabase. Updated `PostApiCareer.ts` to also resolve and save the headshot URL alongside the existing `id_proof` field, and fixed a nested-array bug in `working_areas` where `[selectedArea]` was double-wrapping an array that was already an array, producing `[["Bafal"]]` instead of `["Bafal"]`.

**Real photos in admin booking details:**
`BookingDetails_1.tsx` was displaying five hardcoded sample images (`require('../../../assets/services/...')`) in the Photos section regardless of the booking. Updated `fetchBookingDataAirtable.ts` to parse `add_photos` (a JSON-encoded string array of Cloudinary URLs) into a `photos` string array on each booking object. In `BookingDetails_1.tsx`, replaced the static `photos` array with `const photoUrls: string[] = booking?.photos || []`, changed all `source={image}` references to `source={{ uri: url }}`, and wrapped the entire Photos section in `{photoUrls.length > 0 && (...)}` so it is hidden when no photos were uploaded.

**Success screen auto-dismiss (2 seconds):**
The booking verified screen (`BookingVerify.tsx`) stayed on screen indefinitely waiting for user interaction. Added `useEffect(() => { const t = setTimeout(() => router.replace('/Home'), 2000); return () => clearTimeout(t); }, [])` to auto-navigate to Home after 2 seconds. Also changed the existing 5-second timeout in `otpVerifiedHB.tsx` to 2 seconds for consistency.

**Logout button removed from Admin Login:**
Removed the conditionally rendered `{isLoggedIn && <TouchableOpacity onPress={handleLogout}>Logout</TouchableOpacity>}` block and its associated `logoutText` style from `AdminLogin.tsx` per product requirement.

**OTP SMS text standardised:**
Updated the Sparrow SMS text across all four OTP screens to a consistent format. For booking, career, and partnership (where the customer/applicant name is available): `Dear {firstname}, your HomeSewa service booking OTP code is {otp}\n\nThanks for using HomeSewa ( www.homesewa.app )`. The first name is extracted with `String(name).split(' ')[0]`. For helpbox (no name available): the greeting line is omitted and the message begins directly with the OTP code line.

**Professional login provisioning — PIN generation:**
When a professional submits the Career form and OTP is verified, `createCareer` now generates a random 4-digit PIN using `Math.floor(1000 + Math.random() * 9000)`, stores it in `workforce.pin` (new column: `ALTER TABLE workforce ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS pin TEXT`), and also inserts a record into the `admins` table with `role: 'professional'` so the professional can log in through the Admin Login screen. After `createCareer` succeeds, `CareerOTP.tsx` sends a welcome SMS to the professional: `Dear {firstname}, you have been registered as a HomeSewa Professional!\n\nYour Login Details:\nPhone: {phone}\nPIN: {pin}\n\nYou can change your PIN from the login page.\n\nThanks for using HomeSewa\n( www.homesewa.app )`.

**Admin login migrated from hardcoded credentials to Supabase:**
`AdminLogin.tsx` previously validated against hardcoded constants (`DUMMY_ADMIN_PHONE = '9852024365'`, `DUMMY_ADMIN_PIN = '1234'`). I added Supabase to the screen and rewrote `handleSubmit` to query both the `admins` table and the `workforce` table in parallel. If either returns a matching active record with the correct PIN, login succeeds. A new `admins` table was created in Supabase (columns: `id`, `full_name`, `phone`, `pin`, `role`, `status`, `created_at`) with an RLS "Allow all" policy, and the original super-admin credentials were seeded as the first row. On login, `adminTable` ('admins' or 'workforce') is stored in AsyncStorage alongside `adminPhone` so that downstream screens know which table the current user belongs to.

**Reset PIN page — phone number field + Supabase verification:**
`AdminChangePassword.tsx` previously used `AsyncStorage.getItem('adminPhone')` as the identity source and showed no error if the user was not logged in. Redesigned the page to include an explicit phone number input at the top (formatted 5-2-3, matching all other phone fields in the app). On Save, the entered phone and old PIN are verified against both `admins` and `workforce` tables simultaneously. Whichever table(s) return a match have their PIN updated. If neither table matches, an "incorrect" error is shown without updating anything. The Save button shows a "Saving..." disabled state during the async operation.

**Booking acceptance SMS text updated:**
Changed the customer SMS sent when a professional accepts a booking in `BookingDetails_1.tsx` to: `Dear {customerName}, your booking request has been accepted by {professionalName} {professionalPhone}.\n\nThank You for using HomeSewa\n( www.homesewa.app )`. Also migrated `fetchProfessionalName` from the legacy Airtable REST API to a Supabase query on the `workforce` table, so the professional's full name is now pulled from the same database as the rest of the app.

**Work completion OTP flow:**
Added a new two-step completion verification system. In `BookingDetails_2.tsx`, when the professional selects "Completed" from the Work Status dropdown and taps Submit, the app generates a 4-digit OTP and sends it to the customer's phone via Sparrow SMS (`Dear {firstname}, your HomeSewa service is being marked as completed. Your completion OTP is: {otp}. Share this code with the professional to confirm.`). The OTP and booking ID are stored in AsyncStorage under `completionOtp`, and the professional is navigated to the new `WorkCompletionOTP.tsx` screen. There, the professional asks the customer for their OTP and enters it into the 4-box input. On match, `updateBookingStatus(bookingId, 'Completed')` is called and the AsyncStorage key is cleared. A green success screen is shown with the customer's name and a "Back to Bookings" button. For all other status changes (Pending, Cancelled, Dispute), the existing direct-update path is unchanged.

**Learning:**
Today deepened my understanding of how AsyncStorage serves as a lightweight cross-screen state bus in React Native — it is ideal for passing serialised form data between a form screen and its OTP screen without needing a global state manager, because the data only needs to live for the duration of a single user flow and is explicitly cleared after use. The key discipline is always removing the AsyncStorage key after reading it, to avoid stale data from a previous session being loaded if the user navigates back and starts the flow again.

The professional login provisioning work illustrated a recurring architectural theme: every new user-facing capability (logging in as a professional) requires a corresponding database record (a row in the `admins` table with `role: 'professional'`). Getting the data into the right shape at creation time — in `createCareer` — is much cleaner than trying to backfill or look it up dynamically later. Generating the PIN server-side (in the API layer) and returning it to the OTP screen for the SMS is the correct separation of concerns: the API owns the data, the screen owns the communication.

**Skills Gained:**
AsyncStorage — cross-screen data passing with explicit cleanup after use, `clearForm` param pattern for post-navigation state reset · Supabase — multi-table parallel queries with `Promise.all`, dynamic table selection based on user role, PIN generation and storage · React Native UI — 2-second auto-dismiss with `setTimeout` + `useEffect` cleanup, conditional section rendering based on data presence, phone number field in a reset form · SMS flows — welcome SMS with login credentials, work completion OTP with customer confirmation step · Authentication architecture — hardcoded-to-database credential migration, role-based login across two tables, `adminTable` session tagging in AsyncStorage

**Challenge & Solution:**
(1) `working_areas` saving as `[["Bafal"]]` instead of `["Bafal"]`. Root cause: `[data['Preferred Working Area']]` was wrapping an already-array value in another array. Fixed by checking `Array.isArray(rawArea)` and using it directly if true. (2) "Clear Form" not working after OTP navigation. Root cause: the form component's state is preserved across navigation transitions — returning to the screen does not re-mount it. Fixed by passing a `clearForm: 'true'` navigation param and detecting it with `useLocalSearchParams` + `useEffect` in the form component. (3) Work completion OTP session not surviving navigation. Solved by storing `{ otp, bookingId }` in AsyncStorage before navigating to `WorkCompletionOTP`, rather than passing the OTP as a URL param (which would expose it in the navigation stack).

**Message to Mentor:**
Today demonstrated how a single design decision — "OTP verification before form submission" — cascades into multiple coordinated changes across the API layer, the form screens, the OTP screens, the navigation configuration, and the database schema. Getting this right required thinking about the full lifecycle of each user flow: where data is created, how it is passed between screens, when it is consumed, and when it is cleaned up. I found the work completion OTP flow particularly satisfying — it creates a real-world trust mechanism between the customer and the professional, ensuring the booking is only closed when both parties agree the work is done.

### Day 30 — 19 June 2026 (Friday)

**Progress:**
Today's work on HomeSewa focused on three major areas: standardising all outgoing SMS text formats across every OTP and notification flow, building a complete professional push notification system from scratch, and fixing a set of form and keyboard UX issues across the app.

**SMS text format standardisation:**
All five SMS-sending flows were updated to use consistent, professionally worded message templates that include the HomeSewa brand footer.

- **Helpbox OTP (`helpboxOTP.tsx`):** Changed to `Hi, Thank you for submitting help request. Your OTP code is {otp}.\n\nThank You for using HomeSewa\n( www.homesewa.app )`.
- **Booking OTP (`BookingOtp.tsx`):** Changed to `Dear {firstname}, Your Service Booking OTP code is {otp}.\n\nThank You for using HomeSewa\n( www.homesewa.app )`. The first name is extracted with `.split(' ')[0]`.
- **Partnership OTP (`PartnershipOTP.tsx`):** Changed to `Dear {companyname}, Your Partnership OTP code is {otp}.\n\nThank You for using HomeSewa\n( www.homesewa.app )`. The full company name is used (`.trim()` rather than `.split(' ')[0]`) since a company name should not be truncated to one word.
- **Career/Professional OTP (`CareerOTP.tsx`):** Changed to `Dear {firstname}, Your Professional Membership OTP code is {otp}.\n\nThank You for using HomeSewa\n( www.homesewa.app )`. Also fixed a typo — the previous template had `"Parofessional"` instead of `"Professional"`.
- **PIN change confirmation (`AdminChangePassword.tsx`):** Added a new SMS sent after a successful PIN update: `Dear {firstname}, Your HomeSewa PIN has been changed successfully.\n\nIf you did not request this change, please contact us immediately.\n(9852024365)\n\nThank You for using HomeSewa\n( www.homesewa.app )`. The `full_name` column was added to both Supabase select queries (`admins` and `workforce`) so the first name can be extracted for the greeting.

**Professional push notification system — `api/notifications.ts`:**
The HR team specified three notification triggers that must fire when a booking is submitted and when a professional accepts it. All three were implemented using a Supabase query + `include_aliases` approach rather than OneSignal tag-based filters — tag filters depend on tags being correctly set on each device, whereas a Supabase query directly fetches the current phone numbers of matching professionals, then targets them via `include_aliases: { external_id: phones }`. This works regardless of OneSignal tag state and is therefore more reliable.

1. **`notifyProfessionalsInCity(service, city)`** — Queries the `workforce` table for all active professionals whose `positions` array contains the service and whose `preferred_city` matches the booking city. Sends a push notification to all matching phone numbers: `"New {service} booking in {city}. Open HomeSewa to respond."` This covers the "all plumbers in Kathmandu" broadcast requirement.

2. **`pushAreaProfessionals(service, area)`** — Queries the `workforce` table for all active professionals whose `positions` contains the service and whose `working_areas` contains the booking area. Shuffles the results and takes up to 5 at random. Sends a personalised push to each: `"Dear {firstname}, a new {service} service request is available in {area}. Open HomeSewa to accept the booking!"` This replaces the earlier SMS-to-5-area-professionals approach — push notifications are instant, free, and do not require Sparrow SMS credits.

3. **`notifyProfessionalsAccepted(service, city, area, excludePhone)`** — Queries all active city professionals for the service, excludes the professional who accepted (matched by phone number after normalising to 10 digits), and sends a push to all others: `"The {service} job in {area} has been accepted by another professional. Stay active for new bookings on HomeSewa."` This informs remaining professionals they no longer need to respond to that booking.

All notification headings were confirmed to contain no emoji characters, matching the app's professional tone.

**BookingOtp.tsx — wired to `pushAreaProfessionals`:**
Replaced the previous `notifyProfessionals` (OneSignal tag-filtered) and `notifyProfessionalsInCity` (duplicate) calls with a single `pushAreaProfessionals(targetService, targetArea)` call fired immediately after the booking is saved to Supabase. The city-wide broadcast (`notifyProfessionalsInCity`) was intentionally removed from the booking submission path after it was confirmed to cause duplicate notifications alongside the area push.

**BookingDetails_1.tsx — wired to `notifyProfessionalsAccepted`:**
Added `notifyProfessionalsAccepted(bookingService, booking.city, booking.area, adminPhone)` to the `handleAcceptOffer` function, called immediately after the booking status is updated to Accepted. The accepting professional's phone is passed as `excludePhone` so they do not receive the "job no longer available" push. The booking service is extracted as the first comma-separated value from `booking.service` since a booking can list multiple services.

**FCM V1 API — real device push notification fix:**
Push notifications were working in the Android emulator but not on real devices. Investigation confirmed the root cause: Firebase had deprecated the Legacy Cloud Messaging API and the Legacy Server Key was no longer available for new projects. OneSignal was configured with no Firebase credentials, so real-device push delivery was silently failing. Fixed by opening Firebase Console → Project Settings → Service accounts, generating a new Service Account JSON, and uploading it to the OneSignal dashboard under Android (FCM V1). All subsequent real-device tests received push notifications correctly.

**OneSignal tag reliability — `_layout.tsx` and `AdminLogin.tsx`:**
Existing professionals who had logged in before the OneSignal tagging code was added would never have their tags set, since tags are only written on login. Added a `useEffect` in `app/_layout.tsx` that runs on every app launch: it reads `adminPhone` from AsyncStorage and, if present, queries the `workforce` table for that phone's `preferred_city` and `positions`, then calls `OneSignal.login(phone)` and `OneSignal.User.addTag(...)` to refresh all tags. This ensures professional tags stay current even if the original login predated the tagging logic.

Also fixed an `adminTable` bug in `AdminLogin.tsx`: the variable was set as `isAdmin ? 'admins' : 'workforce'`, but since `isAdmin` was checked before `isWorker`, professionals who also appeared in the `admins` table were always getting `adminTable = 'admins'` stored in AsyncStorage. Fixed to `isWorker ? 'workforce' : 'admins'`, which correctly identifies the primary table for each user type.

**Booking details label cleanup — `BookingDetails_1.tsx` and `BookingDetails_2.tsx`:**
Removed `"& Time"` from the "Booking Date & Time" and "Service Date & Time" labels in both booking detail screens, since the app stores and displays only the date portion. Labels updated to "Booking Date" and "Service Starting Date" respectively.

**Approx Days to complete — `fetchBookingDataAirtable.ts`:**
The "Approx Days to Complete" field in `BookingDetails_1.tsx` was previously hardcoded to display "10 Days" for every booking. Fixed by calculating the actual duration in `fetchBookingDataAirtable.ts`:

```js
approxDays: (() => {
  const start = Date.parse(item.starting_date);
  const end = Date.parse(item.service_completion_date);
  if (isNaN(start) || isNaN(end) || end < start) return null;
  return Math.round((end - start) / (1000 * 60 * 60 * 24));
})(),
```

The booking detail screen was updated to render `{booking.approxDays} Day(s)` when the value is present, and `"N/A"` when either date is missing or the range is invalid.

**Keyboard UX fixes — `AdminLogin.tsx` and `AdminChangePassword.tsx`:**
Both screens were using `KeyboardAvoidingView` + inner `ScrollView` which did not reliably scroll the form above the keyboard on Android. Replaced both with `KeyboardAwareScrollView` from `react-native-keyboard-aware-scroll-view` with `keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled"`, `enableOnAndroid`, and `extraScrollHeight={20}`. On `AdminChangePassword.tsx`, the inner `ScrollView` was converted to a plain `View` since the outer `KeyboardAwareScrollView` handles all scrolling.

**Hide placeholder on focus — `Book.tsx` and `AdminChangePassword.tsx`:**
Applied the focus-hide placeholder pattern consistently across all text fields in both screens. For `Book.tsx`, an `activeInput` state tracks which field is focused. Each `TextInput`'s `placeholder` prop uses the pattern `activeInput === 'fieldName' ? '' : 'placeholder text'`, with `onFocus` and `onBlur` callbacks setting and clearing `activeInput`. The same pattern was applied to the phone field in `AdminChangePassword.tsx` — placeholder changed from `"98XXXXXXXX"` to `"98520 24 365"` (matching the app-wide phone format) and hidden when the field is focused.

**Learning:**
The most important architectural lesson today was the difference between tag-based OneSignal targeting and direct `include_aliases` targeting. Tag-based filters rely on tags being correctly set on every device — if a professional has never logged in since the tagging code was added, they have no tags and will never receive a push. `include_aliases` targeting works differently: it queries a fresh list of phone numbers from Supabase at send time and pushes directly to those external IDs, completely bypassing the tag state. For a growing user base where some professionals may have old installs with no tags, `include_aliases` is significantly more reliable.

Also learned that `fetch` does not distinguish HTTP success from HTTP failure at the promise level — this lesson from Day 28 was reinforced today when adding the `notifyProfessionalsAccepted` call. Any notification function that does not explicitly check `response.ok` will silently succeed even if the OneSignal API returns a 4xx or 5xx error.

**Skills Gained:**
OneSignal SDK — `include_aliases: { external_id }` direct targeting, `target_channel: 'push'`, Supabase-driven audience building for reliable delivery · Firebase — FCM V1 Service Account JSON generation and OneSignal upload for real-device delivery · React Native forms — `KeyboardAwareScrollView` with `enableOnAndroid`, focus-driven placeholder clearing with `activeInput` state · Supabase — `contains()` array filter for `positions` and `working_areas` columns, multi-table parallel queries for PIN verification · SMS text design — consistent brand footer, first-name extraction, company name handling

**Challenge & Solution:**
(1) Push notifications working in emulator but not on real devices. Root cause: Firebase Legacy API deprecated — no server key available. Fix: generated a Service Account JSON from Firebase Console and uploaded to OneSignal under FCM V1. (2) Duplicate push notifications on booking submission (city-wide + area-specific both firing). Fix: removed `notifyProfessionalsInCity` from the booking submission path — the area-specific `pushAreaProfessionals` is sufficient and avoids redundant alerts. (3) `adminTable` set incorrectly for professionals. Root cause: `isAdmin` check ran before `isWorker`, so professionals in both tables always got `adminTable = 'admins'`. Fix: changed to `isWorker ? 'workforce' : 'admins'`.

**Message to Mentor:**
Today's work demonstrated how backend data quality and frontend targeting logic must stay in sync — the push notification system only works as intended when the `workforce` table has accurate `positions` and `working_areas` data, and when each professional's OneSignal external ID matches their registered phone. Building the tag-refresh mechanism in `_layout.tsx` and switching to Supabase-driven `include_aliases` targeting were both about making the system robust to real-world conditions: professionals who log in infrequently, apps that update, and databases that change. A notification system that only works in a perfectly clean environment is not a production system.

### Day 31 — 21 June 2026 (Sunday)

**Progress:**
Today's session on HomeSewa covered a significant set of features across the admin booking flow, professional profile management, the navigation drawer, a calendar rendering fix, a new Super Admin History screen, and a back-button navigation correction for all drawer-level pages.

**BookingDetails_2 — compact non-scrollable layout with PDF share:**
Redesigned `BookingDetails_2.tsx` to fit entirely on one screen without a `ScrollView`. The layout uses a `flex: 1` card with `justifyContent: 'space-between'` — the top block holds all booking fields and the bottom block holds the Work Status dropdown and Submit button, ensuring no vertical overflow on any device height. Fields are displayed in a single column in the order: Service, Location, Budget, Booking Date, Starting Date, Ending Date, Approx Days to Complete, Special Request, then the Work Status section. All field `marginBottom` values were reduced to `hp('0.7%')` and dividers to `hp('0.6%')` to keep the layout compact. Added a share icon (`Ionicons name="share-outline"`) in the header row that triggers `handleSharePDF` — a two-step function that first tries `expo-print` + `expo-sharing` for real PDF output (using a full HTML receipt template), and falls back to React Native's built-in `Share` API for plain-text sharing when running in Expo Go where the native print module is unavailable. Both paths are wrapped in separate try-catch blocks so Expo Go silently takes the fallback without any error visible to the user.

**BookingDetails_1 — Ending Date, Approx Days, and share button:**
Added the same `Ending Date` and `Approx Days to Complete` fields to `BookingDetails_1.tsx` directly below the Starting Date row, matching the field order now consistent across all booking screens. Added the same `handleSharePDF` function and share icon to the `BookingDetails_1` header row. Row padding was also reduced (`marginBottom: hp('1%')`, `padding: hp('0.5%')`) to tighten the overall layout.

**BookingDetail.tsx — row spacing reduction:**
Reduced `paddingVertical` on booking summary rows from `hp('1.8%')` to `hp('1%')` to bring the content height in line with the other booking pages.

**Calendar Saturday column fix — `BookingHistory.tsx`:**
The weekly calendar was rendering with the Saturday column missing. Root cause: the grid used `flexWrap: 'wrap'` with `width: '${100/7}%'` per cell — floating-point percentage values caused sub-pixel rounding errors on certain screen widths, causing the 7th column to wrap onto a new line and disappear. Fixed by replacing the flat `flexWrap` grid with an explicit week-row architecture: cells are first collected into a `flatCells` array (leading `null` pads for the first day of week, then day numbers 1–N, then trailing `null` pads to reach a multiple of 7), then sliced into `calendarWeeks` — an array of 7-element arrays — and each week is rendered as a `<View style={{ flexDirection: 'row' }}>` row with `flex: 1` cells. This guarantees exactly 7 columns on every row regardless of screen width or platform-specific rounding behaviour.

**Super Admin History screen — `SuperAdminHistory.tsx`:**
Created a new screen accessible to admins (`adminTable === 'admins'`) via a shield icon button in the `BookingHistory` header. The screen fetches all bookings from Airtable via `fetchBookingsFromAirtable`, refreshes every 15 seconds via `setInterval` inside `useFocusEffect`, and exposes eight date range filter pills: Today, Yesterday, This Week, This Month, 3 Months, 6 Months, 1 Year, All. The `filterByRange` utility function compares each booking's `bookingDate` or `startingDate` against computed range boundaries using a `startOf('day' | 'week' | 'month')` helper. The screen also includes a search bar (by booking ID, name, or phone), a live count label, and uses the same `BookingCard` component and `handlePress` routing logic as `BookingHistory`. The filter pills use the teal `#295C59` / `#E8F4F3` colour scheme, and the matching scheme was back-ported to the filter pills in `BookingHistory` for visual consistency.

**Drawer — professional login state (`CustomDrawer.tsx`):**
Rewrote `CustomDrawer.tsx` to detect login state from `AsyncStorage` (`adminPhone`, `adminTable`) on mount and on every navigation state change via `props.navigation.addListener('state', loadProfile)`. When a professional is logged in (`adminTable === 'workforce'`), the drawer queries Supabase for `full_name` and `photo_url` and displays the profile photo, full name, and phone number in the header gradient instead of the HomeSewa logo and tagline. The following items are hidden when logged in: Book a Service, Join as Professional, Become a Partner. A Change PIN item is shown only when logged in. The "Admin Login" button changes label and icon to "Update Profile" and routes to `/admin/UpdateProfile`. The button uses `router.push(isLoggedIn ? '/admin/UpdateProfile' : '/Admin')`.

**Update Profile page — `UpdateProfile.tsx`:**
Created a complete profile editing page accessible from the drawer after professional login. The page has four main sections:

- **Hero section** — Green gradient background with a tappable circular profile avatar (opens `ImagePicker`, passes selected image to `HeadshotCropModal`, uploads result to Supabase Storage via `uploadToStorage`, saves `photo_url` to the workforce row). Displays full name, phone, UIN badge, Active/Inactive status chip, and preferred city.
- **Personal Information** — Full Name (editable `TextInput`), Phone (read-only), Email (editable), Gender (Male/Female/Other pill selector).
- **Professional Information** — Services/Expertise (from `positions` column), Years of Experience (editable), Preferred City (editable), Preferred Working Area (editable).
- **Account** — Change PIN row that navigates to `/AdminChangePassword`, Save Changes button (uploads photo then updates `full_name`, `email`, `gender`, `preferred_city`, `preferred_area`, `years_experience` in the `workforce` table), and a Logout button with a confirmation `Alert` that clears `adminPhone` and `adminTable` from AsyncStorage and navigates to Home.

Added `admin/UpdateProfile` as a hidden screen in `app/(drawer)/_layout.tsx` with `drawerItemStyle: { display: 'none' }`.

**Back button fix — `Header3drawer.tsx`:**
Drawer-level pages (Career, Glossary, Partnership, FAQs) were sending users to the FAQs screen when the back button was pressed, because `router.back()` was navigating to whatever page was previously in the navigation stack — often FAQs, since it is listed before those items in the drawer and may have been visited in the same session. Fixed by adding an optional `goHome` boolean prop to `Header3drawer`. When `goHome` is `true`, the back button calls `router.replace('/Home')` directly instead of `router.back()`. The prop was added to `Career.tsx`, `Glossary.tsx`, `Partnership.tsx`, and `FAQs.tsx` as `<Header3 goHome />`. OTP sub-pages (`CareerOTP`, `PartnershipOTP`, `helpboxOTP`, `otpVerifiedHB`) do not pass the prop, so they continue using `router.back()` to return the user to their parent form, which is the correct behaviour for a multi-step flow.

**Learning:**
The calendar Saturday bug was a textbook example of why pixel-perfect layout mathematics cannot be trusted in cross-platform environments. A `width` of `${100/7}%` is `14.285714...%` — a repeating decimal that every rendering engine rounds differently. The correct approach for any fixed-column grid in React Native is `flex: 1` inside a fixed-direction `flexDirection: 'row'` row, which lets the layout engine distribute space proportionally with no floating-point arithmetic. This pattern is now applied to the calendar and I will use it by default for any future column-based grid.

The `goHome` prop pattern in `Header3drawer` illustrates an important React component design principle: a shared component can support multiple behaviours through explicit opt-in props without branching on context or global state. The caller that needs non-default behaviour opts in; all other callers are unaffected. This is preferable to reading navigation state or route name inside the header component, which would create hidden coupling between the header and the pages that use it.

The `CustomDrawer` `photo_url` fix reinforced the importance of verifying column names against the actual database schema before writing query code. Using `headshot_url` (an assumed name) in the Supabase select caused the profile photo to silently return `null` on every load — no error was thrown, just missing data. Printing or logging the raw Supabase response before binding it to state is the fastest way to catch column name mismatches.

**Skills Gained:**
React Native layout — `flex: 1` column grids as a replacement for `flexWrap` percentage grids, explicit week-row calendar rendering · PDF / share — `expo-print` + `expo-sharing` with graceful Expo Go fallback via `try-catch` dynamic `require()` and React Native `Share` API · Supabase — profile photo upload with `uploadToStorage`, row update for multiple professional fields, parallel `Promise.all` queries for multi-table login · React Native component design — optional `goHome` prop for shared header back-button behaviour · Expo Router — hidden drawer screens with `drawerItemStyle: { display: 'none' }`, `useFocusEffect` with `setInterval` for periodic data refresh

**Challenge & Solution:**
(1) `expo-print` and `expo-sharing` throw "Cannot find native module" in Expo Go — the native modules are only available in real/dev-client builds. Fixed by wrapping each `require()` in a separate `try-catch`: the production path attempts the PDF route, and if it throws (Expo Go), falls through to the React Native `Share` API text fallback with no user-visible error. (2) Calendar Saturday column missing. Root cause: `width: '${100/7}%'` percentage rounding causing the 7th cell to wrap to a new line. Fixed by building explicit 7-element week arrays and rendering each as a `flexDirection: 'row'` view with `flex: 1` cells. (3) Drawer back button going to FAQs instead of Home. Root cause: `router.back()` navigates to whatever was last in the navigation stack, which was FAQs when users visited it before opening Career or Glossary. Fixed by adding `goHome` prop to `Header3drawer` and using `router.replace('/Home')` on the drawer-level pages. (4) Profile photo not showing in drawer. Root cause: the Supabase `select` query used `headshot_url` but the actual column is `photo_url`. Fixed by correcting the column name in both the select call and the `data.photo_url` access.

**Message to Mentor:**
Today's session connected several systems that had previously worked in isolation — the booking flow, the professional profile, the navigation drawer, and the admin history view — into a coherent professional experience. The work completion OTP flow, the drawer login state, and the UpdateProfile page together mean a professional can now log in, update their information, manage bookings, and confirm work completion entirely within the app. The back-button fix was a small change in terms of lines of code but a significant improvement in navigation consistency — users who open drawer pages from any context now always return to Home, which is the expected behaviour for top-level pages. I am increasingly focusing on cross-page consistency as a quality standard alongside individual feature correctness.

### Day 32 — 22 June 2026 (Monday)

**Progress:**
Today's work on HomeSewa covered three main areas: a full redesign of the Help Box management screen to match the approved mockup, a major polish pass on the Update Profile form to match the Career (Join as Professional) form's field components and visual style, and a targeted bug-fixing session that addressed one silent logic bug, two crash-level navigation registration gaps, and a Nepal-timezone date mismatch in the booking calendar.

**Help Box redesign — `HelpBox.tsx`:**
The previous Help Box implementation used a card-per-message layout with inline reply and close actions. The HR team shared a new mockup specifying a table-based layout. I rewrote the screen completely to match it. The new page has four sections stacked vertically:

- **Search bar** — A bordered `TextInput` with a search icon, filtering live by `sender_phone` as the user types. A close icon clears the query.
- **Duration dropdown** — A labelled dropdown button (Today / Yesterday / This Week / This Month / 3 Months / 6 Months / 1 Year / All) that applies a date-range filter to the `created_at` column. The filter logic uses local-time `startOf` helpers for day, week, and month boundaries — consistent with the timezone fix applied to `BookingHistory.tsx` today.
- **Status dropdown** — A second dropdown (All / Solved / Open) filtering by the `status` column. All three filter states are combined in a single `useMemo` so any combination of search, duration, and status filters always produces the correct result set without redundant Supabase round-trips.
- **Table** — A white card with a teal `#295C59` header row (Ticket ID / Phone Number / Status) and alternating-colour data rows. The Status column renders a small circular badge: a green `checkmark` icon on a `#dcfce7` background for `solved`, and a red `close` icon on a `#fee2e2` background for `open`. Tapping any row navigates to `HelpBoxDetail` with the full record serialised as a JSON route param.

A refresh button in the header re-fetches from Supabase on demand.

**Help Box detail page — `HelpBoxDetail.tsx`:**
Created a new detail screen that receives the help message record via route param. The page shows three white cards: Sender (name and phone), Message (full text), and Reply (editable `TextInput` pre-filled with any existing reply). For open tickets, two action buttons are rendered: "Save Draft" (saves the reply text and keeps status as `open`) and "Mark as Solved" (saves reply and sets status to `solved`). A green confirmation banner replaces the buttons when the ticket is already solved, and the reply input becomes read-only. Registered the screen in `app/(drawer)/admin/_layout.tsx`.

**Update Profile — Services / Expertise, Preferred City, Preferred Working Area (`UpdateProfile.tsx`):**
The three professional information fields were previously using a custom inline tag-search implementation (for expertise) and plain `TextInput` fields (for city and area). Replaced all three with the `DropdownAdd` component — the same component used in the Career (Join as Professional) form — using identical data sources (`servicesData2` from `ServiceData.ts`, and `city` / `area` from `Data.ts`). Props were matched exactly to the Career form: `placeholderColor="#4B4B4B"`, `maxSelections={5}` for expertise and area, `maxSelections={1}` for city. The city dropdown resets the area selection on change, matching Career's behaviour. The `area` state was changed from `string` to `string[]` with backward-compatible loading logic that handles both array and comma-separated string values from Supabase.

**Update Profile — Career form visual parity (`UpdateProfile.tsx`):**
After the dropdown fields were updated, a broader styling pass brought all form fields in line with the Career form's visual language:

- **Labels** — Changed from the custom `#5A7270` / `scaleFont(12)` style to `color: '#4A4A4A'`, `fontSize: wp('3.6%')`, `fontWeight: '600'`, `paddingLeft: 4` — identical to Career's `styles.label`.
- **TextInputs** — Changed `borderColor` from `#D6E8E7` to `#E2E8F0`, matching Career's border. Added `inputActive` style (`borderColor: '#295C59'`, `backgroundColor: '#EFF8F7'`) applied on focus, using an `activeInput` state string wired to `onFocus` / `onBlur` on every `TextInput` — the same pattern Career uses.
- **Gender field** — Replaced the three pill-button selectors with radio buttons (circular outer ring / filled inner dot) in the Career style (`radioOuter`, `radioInner`, `radioLabel`), supporting Male / Female / Other.
- **Placeholder text** — Changed all placeholder text colours from `#B0BEC5` to `#4B4B4B` to match Career.

**Bug fixes:**

1. **Booking calendar showing 0 bookings on date tap — `BookingHistory.tsx`:** When a user selected a date on the calendar, `filteredData` still applied the active status filter (e.g. "New / Open"). If the selected date only had Completed or Cancelled bookings, those were excluded and the screen showed "0 Bookings" even though the count dot on the calendar cell correctly showed 1 or more. Fixed by short-circuiting `filteredData` when `selectedDate` is set — the date view now returns all bookings for that date regardless of the current status filter. The status filter continues to apply normally in the list view (no date selected).

2. **Missing screen registrations causing navigation crashes — `admin/_layout.tsx`:** The `SuperAdminHistory` and `WorkCompletionOTP` screens existed as files and were navigated to from `BookingHistory.tsx` and `BookingDetails_2.tsx` respectively, but were not registered in the admin `Stack` navigator. Any navigation to these screens would crash at runtime with a "No route found" error. Added both `<Stack.Screen>` entries to resolve the crash.

3. **Timezone mismatch in booking date filtering — `BookingHistory.tsx`:** The `parseToYMD` and `todayYMD` functions were using `.toISOString().split('T')[0]` to extract the date portion. `toISOString()` converts the date to UTC before formatting — in Nepal (UTC+5:45), a booking timestamped at e.g. 8 PM UTC appears as the following calendar day locally, causing the calendar dot and the filter to disagree on which date a booking belongs to. Fixed both functions to use `.getFullYear()`, `.getMonth()`, and `.getDate()` (local time) with manual zero-padding, so the extracted date always matches the Nepal local calendar.

4. **Unused `FlatList` import — `HelpBox.tsx`:** `FlatList` was imported but the component uses `ScrollView` throughout. Removed the unused import.

**Learning:**
The booking calendar filter bug illustrated an important distinction between two layers of filtering in the same screen: the calendar date dot uses `countsByDate` (which counts all bookings regardless of status), while `filteredData` applies both date and status filters. If these two layers are not kept in sync, the calendar can show a booking exists on a date while the list below shows zero — a contradiction that is immediately visible and confusing to the user. The fix was to treat a calendar date selection as an exclusive filter mode that disables all other filters, which is also the more intuitive UX: when you tap a date, you want to see everything that happened that day, not just the subset matching your current status pill.

The Career form visual parity work reinforced the principle that a consistent visual language across all screens of the same type — form inputs, labels, active states — reduces cognitive load for the user and signals professionalism. Users should not have to re-learn the visual affordances of each form. `borderColor: '#E2E8F0'` on rest and `borderColor: '#295C59'` on focus is now the single standard for all text inputs in the app.

**Skills Gained:**
React Native UI — `DropdownAdd` multi-select with `maxSelections`, Career form visual parity (label style, input border/active state, radio buttons), table layout with alternating row colours and badge status icons · State management — `useMemo` combining three filter dimensions (search, duration, status), `string[]` vs `string` backward-compatible loading from Supabase · Bug diagnosis — calendar filter layer mismatch, timezone offset in `.toISOString()`, navigator registration gaps, unused import cleanup · Expo Router — `<Stack.Screen>` registration for sub-screens of a nested admin Stack

**Challenge & Solution:**
(1) Help Box table Status column needed two visually distinct states. Solution: small circular `View` badges with Ionicons `checkmark` / `close` icons and green / red background colours — compact, immediately scannable, and consistent with common mobile UI conventions. (2) `area` state in UpdateProfile was `string` but `DropdownAdd` requires `string[]`. Solution: changed state type to `string[]`, updated Supabase load to parse both array and comma-string formats for backward compatibility, and updated the Supabase update to save the array directly. (3) Nepal-timezone date mismatch in `parseToYMD`. Solution: replaced `toISOString()` with local-time getters and manual zero-padding — a simple change that makes date extraction timezone-aware by construction.

**Message to Mentor:**
Today's session was a good reminder that the most impactful work is not always the most visible. The calendar bug fix, timezone correction, and missing screen registrations were small changes in terms of lines of code, but each one addressed a real failure mode that would frustrate a professional using the app in the field. The HelpBox redesign and UpdateProfile polish are also about trust — a super admin managing support tickets needs a clear, scannable view, and a professional updating their profile deserves the same quality of form experience as the career application form they originally used to join. Consistency and correctness compound over time into a product that feels genuinely well-made.

### Day 33 — 24 June 2026 (Tuesday)

**Progress:**
Today's work on HomeSewa focused on three areas: HelpBox UI polish, a confirmation flow improvement for the Help request button, and the full Android production build pipeline — diagnosing a Gradle build failure, fixing the root cause, and successfully producing a signed AAB for the Google Play Store.

**HelpBox page — header removal and status icon refinement:**
Removed the custom "← Help Box" green header row that appeared between the `Header4` nav bar and the search section. The header was redundant — the page title was already shown in the top nav bar and the back arrow was duplicated. With the custom header removed, the page flows directly from the nav bar into the search bar, giving the content more vertical space.

The Status column badge was changed from a red close icon (`Ionicons name="close"`, `#fee2e2` background) to an amber clock icon (`Ionicons name="time-outline"`, `#fef3c7` background) for open tickets. A close/X icon implies the user can dismiss something — not appropriate for an "open" ticket status. A clock icon correctly communicates "pending / awaiting response". Solved tickets retain the green checkmark badge, so the two states are now visually distinct and semantically accurate.

**Help request confirmation dialog — `NumberBar.tsx`:**
The Help button on the Home page previously navigated directly to the OTP screen the moment it was tapped, with no opportunity to reconsider. Added a confirmation `Alert` between button press and navigation:

> **Confirm Help Request**
> Send a help request from +977 9852020060? Our team will contact you shortly.
> `Cancel` · `Confirm`

Only after tapping Confirm does the app navigate to `helpboxOTP`. This is consistent with the "Mark as Solved" confirmation already in place on `HelpBoxDetail` — any irreversible or externally visible action should give the user a chance to cancel.

**EAS Build — owner and project ID correction:**
The `app.json` had `"owner": "mr.ded"` and an outdated `projectId` from the original project setup. Updated both to match the SRIYOG EAS account: `"owner": "sriyog"` and `"projectId": "b04b2dce-e9db-4925-b32c-498453c6031d"`. Without this correction, any EAS build would target the wrong project and upload credentials to the wrong account.

**EAS Build — Gradle failure diagnosis:**
Triggered an EAS preview build (APK profile) which failed at the `Run gradlew` phase with a generic "Gradle build failed with unknown error." The build log URL was retrieved via `eas build:view --json` and the full log was downloaded to analyse the failure. Key finding from the log:

```
No environment variables with visibility "Plain text" and "Sensitive"
found for the "preview" environment on EAS.
```

Cross-referencing this with the `package.json` `eas-build-post-install` script:

```bash
echo $GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON_BASE64 | base64 -d > android/app/google-services.json
```

And the `.gitignore`:

```
google-services.json
android/app/google-services.json
```

Root cause confirmed: `google-services.json` is excluded from git (and therefore excluded from the EAS upload archive). The build server relies on the `eas-build-post-install` script to restore it from a base64-encoded secret — but that secret (`GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON_BASE64`) had never been added to the EAS project. With no `google-services.json` present, Firebase's Gradle plugin could not configure the build, causing the Gradle failure.

**EAS Build — secret added and AAB produced:**
Generated the base64 value of `android/app/google-services.json` using PowerShell:

```powershell
$bytes = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes("android\app\google-services.json")
$b64 = [Convert]::ToBase64String($bytes)
$b64 | Out-File -FilePath "encoded.txt" -Encoding ascii -NoNewline
```

Added it to EAS as a project-scoped secret:

```bash
eas secret:create --scope project --name GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON_BASE64 --value $b64 --type string
```

Then triggered the production build profile (which outputs `.aab` rather than `.apk`):

```bash
eas build --platform android --profile production --non-interactive
```

The build completed successfully. The signed AAB is available at:
`https://expo.dev/accounts/sriyog/projects/homesewa/builds/776cda2e-a8d3-49d2-9851-c8f46a2258be`

**GitHub push — both repositories:**
Committed all changes and pushed to both the personal repo (`https://github.com/Samek55/HomeSewa`) and the organisation repo (`https://github.com/SRIYOGconsulting/HomeSewa`).

**Learning:**
The Gradle failure diagnosis reinforced a critical lesson about EAS bare-workflow builds: EAS does not implicitly include gitignored files in the build archive. Any file that is both required by the native build and excluded from git must be restored by the `eas-build-post-install` script using a secret environment variable. If the secret is missing, the script runs silently (the `echo` call succeeds but writes an empty file), and the failure only surfaces later in the Gradle phase — making the root cause non-obvious. Checking the EAS log for "No environment variables found" before investigating the Gradle output is the correct first diagnostic step for any Firebase-related Gradle failure in a bare Expo workflow.

Also learned that `certutil` (Windows command for base64 encoding) uses `&&` chaining — not available in PowerShell 5.1. The correct PowerShell alternative is to use `[Convert]::ToBase64String` on the raw bytes, which is more reliable anyway as it produces a single clean base64 string with no line breaks or headers.

**Skills Gained:**
EAS Build — bare workflow secret management, `GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON_BASE64` pattern for gitignored Firebase config, build profile selection (`preview` vs `production`), build log retrieval via `eas build:view --json` · PowerShell — `[System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes` + `[Convert]::ToBase64String` for cross-platform base64 encoding · React Native UI — semantically correct status icon selection (clock vs close), redundant header removal · UX patterns — confirmation Alert for externally visible actions

**Challenge & Solution:**
(1) EAS Gradle build failed with no visible error message. Root cause: `GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON_BASE64` secret not set on EAS — the `eas-build-post-install` script could not restore `google-services.json`, so Firebase's Gradle plugin found no config file and aborted. Fix: encode `android/app/google-services.json` to base64 with PowerShell and add it as an EAS project secret. (2) `certutil && findstr` command failed in PowerShell 5.1 because `&&` is not a valid statement separator. Fix: replaced with PowerShell-native `[Convert]::ToBase64String` which produces a clean single-line base64 string directly.

**Message to Mentor:**
Today's build pipeline work was a good example of how a silent configuration gap — a missing secret — can produce a confusing, misdirected error many steps downstream. The Gradle phase failing makes it look like a code or dependency problem, but the real cause was an infrastructure step that silently did nothing. Developing the habit of checking environment variable availability first — before reading Gradle logs — saved significant time. Successfully producing the first signed HomeSewa AAB ready for Play Store upload is a meaningful milestone for the project.
